# Hermes Desktop ↔ Talaria — parity contract

> [!WARNING]
> This row ledger is a historical 2026-08-17/18 snapshot. Its percentages and
> source line citations are not current Hermes parity claims. New work is
> governed by the exact upstream pin and completion rules in
> [`docs/PARITY-BASELINE.md`](docs/PARITY-BASELINE.md) and the current-state
> ledger in [`docs/HERMES-CURRENT-AUDIT.md`](docs/HERMES-CURRENT-AUDIT.md).
> In particular, the coverage table, area introductions, and “Next 25” below
> must not be used to choose work: they predate Settings, liveness reconciliation,
> cron management, pairing administration, multiline mentions, shared rooms,
> source-qualified Command Center, and Advanced Terminal.

**Snapshot: 2026-08-17** (spot-checks re-verified 2026-08-18; **30 rows moved by the 2026-08-18 overnight run**, see [below](#the-2026-08-18-overnight-run)) · **1,190 rows** across 10 areas · every row cites a desktop `file:line`.

> **Bot Mode has its own, deeper contract** in [`docs/BOT-MODE-PARITY.md`](docs/BOT-MODE-PARITY.md) — roster rows, canonical chat, handles/mentions, A2A delegation, group chats and the bot CRUD surface are enumerated there at the same granularity. This document covers the ten *app* areas and touches Bot Mode only where it shows up in the shared shell (the `profiles-botmode` section below).

## Purpose

This is the working parity contract between **Hermes Desktop** (the Electron + React client) and **Talaria** (the iOS client). It exists so that three questions have one answer each:

1. **Does Talaria do this?** — every row is a single, testable desktop behaviour with a status.
2. **Where is it on each side?** — every row cites the desktop source that defines the behaviour, and the Talaria source that implements it (or nothing, when it does not).
3. **Should it ever?** — rows that cannot cross the platform boundary are marked ➖ and collected in [Mobile ceiling](#mobile-ceiling), with the reason and the iOS analogue, so nobody burns a week chasing a hover tooltip.

It is deliberately granular. "Chat works" is not a parity statement; "tapping a sent message opens an inline edit composer that truncates and resubmits the turn" is. There are 1,190 such statements here.

### How this was audited

Ten agents read the **real desktop tree** — 1,387 renderer files under `apps/desktop/src` and 205 files under the Electron main process — not a summary, not a feature list, not the docs. Each agent owned one area, walked the components and stores that implement it, and emitted one row per behaviour with the defining `file:line`. Talaria was read the same way, from `app/Packages/Talaria/Sources/` (TalariaKit / TalariaUI / TalariaTheme).

**Freshness caveat, and what was done about it.** The audit ran while a large parity pass was still landing, so a slice of it was stale on arrival. Every row whose feature sounded like it might now exist was re-read against the current Swift before this document was written; **48 rows were corrected** and are marked `CORRECTED — landed <date>` in their Notes. The largest cluster is the model picker: `ModelEffortSheet.swift`, `AppModelLive+Models.swift` and `GatewayClient+Models.swift` were rewritten to desktop's `model-catalog-menu.tsx` shape *after* the models-providers agent had already reported — 45 of that area's 97 rows moved up a rung or straight to ✅. The other three corrections are one chat row and two voice settings. Anything not marked CORRECTED is as the audit found it.

Corrected rows were verified by reading the implementation, not by grepping for a symbol name. Where a wire call exists but nothing calls it (`model.save_key`, `model.disconnect`), the row stays ⭕ and the note says the RPC is already ported — that is an effort estimate, not a status.

<a id="the-2026-08-18-overnight-run"></a>

### The 2026-08-18 overnight run

Roadmap phases 0–5 landed in one unattended session. **30 rows moved** (26 → 🔶, 4 → ✅) and five more had their Notes corrected without a status change; every one of them carries the date and a Talaria `file:line`.

One rule decided all thirty, and it is stricter than the legend alone would be: **no gateway was reachable during the run.** So a row is ✅ only where the behaviour is device-local or client-only and the build plus a read of the implementation prove it (the Settings screen existing, the artifact zoom viewer, copy-a-path, the app's own version). Everything whose correctness depends on a gateway's actual response shape is **🔶 with the words "built but never run against a live gateway"**, however complete the code looks — cron editing and run history, pairing, provider keys, `approvals.timeout`, the voice catalog, and the whole `session.active_list` liveness path. Those notes are a to-do list for the first session on a real gateway, not hedging: the parse follows the upstream Python, but a shape read from source and a shape seen on the wire are different claims and this document does not blur them.

### Status legend

| | Status | Means |
| :-: | --- | --- |
| ✅ | **present** | Talaria implements the behaviour. Shape may differ (a sheet where desktop has a hover menu); the user-visible capability is there. |
| 🔶 | **partial** | Some of it works. The row says exactly which half is missing — a weaker scope, a read-only view of something desktop lets you edit, or a device-local stand-in for a synced setting. |
| ⭕ | **missing** | Not implemented. Nothing in `Sources/` does this. |
| ➖ | **n/a-mobile** | Cannot or should not cross to iOS. Hover affordances, multi-window placement, local process management, hardware-keyboard-only paths. Collected with reasons in [Mobile ceiling](#mobile-ceiling). |

Two conventions worth knowing before reading a table:

- **Effort · value** is one column: `M · critical` means a medium-sized job of critical value. Effort is S / M / L / XL as sized by the auditing agent against the Talaria codebase; value is how much a phone-first operator loses without it, which is *not* the same as how much a desktop user would.
- **Surface** names the wire the behaviour rides — an RPC, a REST route, an event, or `client-only` when it never leaves the renderer. `client-only` rows need no gateway work; RPC rows may need backend support that already exists.

### Coverage

| Area | ✅ | 🔶 | ⭕ | ➖ | Rows | Portable | Covered |
| --- | --: | --: | --: | --: | --: | --: | --: |
| [Chat & transcript](#chat-transcript) | 28 | 24 | 74 | 9 | 135 | 126 | 32% |
| [Sidebars & navigation](#sidebars-nav) | 14 | 25 | 49 | 26 | 114 | 88 | 30% |
| [Models & providers](#models-providers) | 29 | 18 | 45 | 5 | 97 | 92 | 41% |
| [Capabilities, skills & MCP](#capabilities-skills-mcp) | 19 | 11 | 52 | 6 | 88 | 82 | 30% |
| [Settings](#settings) | 20 | 31 | 121 | 30 | 202 | 172 | 21% |
| [Profiles & Bot Mode shell](#profiles-botmode) | 20 | 23 | 62 | 6 | 111 | 105 | 30% |
| [Cron, subagents & artifacts](#cron-agents-artifacts) | 14 | 27 | 55 | 5 | 101 | 96 | 29% |
| [Messaging, webhooks & overlays](#messaging-webhooks-overlays) | 6 | 20 | 54 | 25 | 105 | 80 | 20% |
| [Gateway, store & hooks](#gateway-store-hooks) | 29 | 24 | 22 | 8 | 83 | 75 | 55% |
| [Electron & native shell](#electron-native) | 19 | 9 | 57 | 69 | 154 | 85 | 28% |
| **Overall** | **198** | **212** | **591** | **189** | **1190** | **1001** | **30%** |

*Covered* counts a 🔶 as half a ✅ over the portable rows only (➖ excluded from the denominator — a phone is not failing to be a Mac). Read it as a direction of travel, not a grade: the areas at the bottom are mostly there because desktop has a Settings screen with 202 discrete controls and Talaria has no settings screen at all, which is one decision, not 130 of them.

### The shape of the gap, in one paragraph

Historical snapshot only: the paragraph that originally occupied this section
predated the Settings shell, session verbs, cron editing/history, provider and
pairing administration, source-qualified Git/Files/Projects controls,
`session.active_list` reseeding, network recovery, and runtime reaping. The
current remaining product clusters are rich transcript/session depth; deeper
messaging, memory, subagent and provider management; artifact pagination and
unlocked-root exceptions; and Git/System depth. Live/device certification remains open for
push, shared rooms, Advanced Terminal, and the row-specific gateway paths in
[`TESTING.md`](TESTING.md).

### Chat & transcript

`chat-transcript` · 135 rows — ✅ 28 · 🔶 24 · ⭕ 74 · ➖ 9

Historical row summary, corrected for current source: Talaria now includes a
full-width growing mention composer, edit, rewind/restore, regenerate,
jump-to-bottom, artifact preview/share, and quiet/advanced tool presentation.
Remaining transcript depth is persistent multimodal/generated-media parts,
grouped live tool runs, diff/ANSI/search/math/diagram renderers, per-message TTS
and whole-turn timing, durable prompt queue, alternative-branch navigation, and
lineage presentation. The row statuses below
remain snapshot counts unless individually marked with a current correction.

<a id="sidebars-nav"></a>

### Sidebars & navigation

`sidebars-nav` · 114 rows — ✅ 14 · 🔶 25 · ⭕ 49 · ➖ 26

Desktop's left sidebar is a five-row nav rail + FTS session search + Pinned section + a Sessions section that is simultaneously a flat recents list, a 4-way grouping engine (date/project/status/profile), a 6-key sort, a 6-flag row-metadata system, a 4-axis filter stack (status/PR/profile/project), a drill-in project/repo/worktree browser, per-platform messaging groups and a cron section — plus a bottom profile rail, a right sidebar (files/terminal/review), a titlebar tool cluster and a ~14-item statusbar. Talaria collapses all of this into a 5-tab bar (Home/Activity/Approvals/A2A/Artifacts) over a flat bot (=profile) roster, with a per-bot SessionsSheet and a global SearchPalette. Genuinely present: bot roster rows with live status/unread badges, per-bot session list with local + FTS search, swipe rename/delete, tap-to-resume, branch/compress/export overflow, cross-session search palette, artifacts tab, connections push, deep links. Materially missing: pinning, archiving, per-session unread watermark, ALL grouping/sort/filter/row-metadata controls, projects/repos/worktrees entirely, messaging platform groups, a global (cross-bot) session list, cron/routines as a top-level destination, mark-read controls, move-to-project, per-session color, copy-id, open-in-terminal/tab/window/split, load-more paging, and the entire statusbar readout family (context gauge, timers, approval-mode, gateway health panel, versions). The single biggest structural gap is that Talaria has no cross-bot session list at all — SessionsSheet is per-bot and reachable only from the search palette's "Sessions…" action (nothing posts .talariaOpenSessions; RootView.swift:182 has no producer), so a phone-first user cannot see "what are all my agents doing" as a list of sessions the way the desktop sidebar shows it. PARITY.md §4/§130 is stale: it claims listSessions has zero UI callers, but SessionsSheet.swift now drives session.list, rename, delete and FTS live.

<a id="models-providers"></a>

### Models & providers

`models-providers` · 97 rows — ✅ 29 · 🔶 18 · ⭕ 45 · ➖ 5

Desktop's model/provider surface is three distinct systems: (1) the composer catalog menu — searchable, provider-grouped, `-fast`-family-collapsed, with a per-row hover submenu for thinking/effort/fast, a per-model preset memory, Refresh Models, and an Edit Models shortlist curator; (2) the full picker dialog which adds pricing ($/Mtok, discounts, FREE), Nous free/pro tier badges and locked Pro rows; (3) provider onboarding + Settings→Model/Providers — OAuth pkce/device_code/external, API-key catalog, local endpoints, recommended-default confirm, aux-task models, MoA presets, fallback chains and stale-aux/cron-impact guards.

> **Audit note — this area was re-verified on 2026-08-18 and 45 of its 97 rows were corrected.** Everything the original summary said about Talaria's picker described the sheet as it existed *before* `ModelEffortSheet.swift`, `AppModelLive+Models.swift` and `GatewayClient+Models.swift` were rewritten later the same evening. What actually ships now is system (1) nearly whole plus the pricing half of system (2): provider-grouped sections sorted by display name with `slug · total_models` headings and per-provider warnings, a search field spanning name/slug/id/alias, `-fast` family collapsing, date-pinned snapshot dedupe, per-model capability gating, all seven reasoning levels plus a thinking-off chip, a Fast toggle that resolves both mechanisms (`speed=fast` param vs a `…-fast` sibling id), a persisted per-model preset memory keyed `provider::model`, optimistic writes with rollback and a notice banner, the expensive-model `confirm_required` handshake, deferred mid-turn switches, `$/Mtok` pricing with FREE and Pro-locked rows, Refresh Models, and an Edit Models curator with the hide-all sentinel. What genuinely remains: the **composer pill** still prints a raw model id (the label helpers exist and are simply not called there), free-tier/Pro **provider** badges and discount pricing are parsed but unrendered, and the entire **provider onboarding / credential** third of the area (OAuth PKCE, device code, API-key catalog, custom endpoints, aux-task slots, MoA management, fallback chains) is untouched — though `model.save_key` and `model.disconnect` are already ported and simply have no caller.

| Feature | Desktop source | Surface | Talaria | Effort · value | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Pinned-override dot on the pill when a manual pick overrides the profile default on a fresh draft | `app/chat/composer/model-pill.tsx:86-87,103-110` | client-only | ⭕ | M · high | Exists specifically because a forgotten paid-model pick cost users money (#62055). Talaria has no manual-vs-default model source distinction at all. |
| Search-term highlighting inside model and provider labels | `app/shell/model-catalog-menu.tsx:386,453`<br>`components/ui/highlight-matches.tsx` | client-only | ⭕ | S · low | Depends on search existing first. |
| Collapse/expand a provider's group, persisted across launches | `app/shell/model-catalog-menu.tsx:373-393`<br>`store/provider-collapse.ts:22-27` | client-only (localStorage) | ⭕ | S · medium | Requires grouping first. On a phone screen this matters more than on desktop. |
| Editing options on a NON-active model row records a preference without switching model | `app/shell/model-menu-panel.tsx:199-219` | client-only + config.set when active | ⭕ `TalariaUI/Screens/ModelEffortSheet.swift:466-475` | M · low | Still missing, but half-built: a non-active row now READS its remembered preset for its status label. The controls underneath act only on the active model, so recording a preference for a model you are not using still requires switching to it. |

<a id="capabilities-skills-mcp"></a>

### Capabilities, skills & MCP

`capabilities-skills-mcp` · 88 rows — ✅ 19 · 🔶 11 · ⭕ 52 · ➖ 6

Desktop's Capabilities surface is three tabs (Skills / Tools / MCP) plus the ambient MCP machinery (composer suggestion pills, the inline setup_mcp consent card, a 30-minute background health sweep, an OS deep-link installer) and a Plugins settings page. Talaria collapses all of it into one scrolling CapabilitiesView with four sections. The skeleton is genuinely there and honest — skills toggle, toolset pin, MCP add/remove/probe/OAuth/API-key, plugin toggle — and Talaria is actually ahead of desktop in two places (a real add-server form instead of raw JSON, and mcp.servers.set_api_key without hand-editing). But the depth is missing almost everywhere: no skill detail/SKILL.md view, no skill edit or archive, no usage counts, no sort or bulk actions, no Skills Hub at all (browse/install/update-all — all reachable over skills.manage which Talaria already calls with action:"list"), no toolset provider/backend configuration whatsoever (env keys, post-setup installers, model catalogs, web search-vs-extract split, terminal backend), no MCP server enable/disable toggle, no per-tool gating, no paste-import, no logs pane, no cost overlay, and critically no `reload.mcp` after any MCP write — so a server added from the phone never reaches the running agent until the session restarts. Three ambient surfaces are entirely absent and matter most to a phone-first user: the background MCP health sweep that catches expired OAuth, the composer suggestion pills that connect a vendor from a pasted link, and a working setup_mcp card — Talaria's version can only say "I'll do it on desktop" or "no", which is a hard-coded admission of non-parity in the payload the agent reads. Highest-value closes, in order: reload.mcp write-through (S), a real setup_mcp approve path (M), MCP server enable toggle + per-tool gating (M), skill detail/edit/archive (M), Skills Hub browse+install (M), toolset provider config (XL).

| Feature | Desktop source | Surface | Talaria | Effort · value | Notes |

Desktop toggles any skill regardless of provenance. |
| Skill row shows lifetime usage count badge (×N) | `app/skills/index.tsx:736-738` | REST /api/skills (SkillInfo.usage) | ⭕ | M · medium | Talaria's SkillEntry has no usage field at all (GatewayClient+Capabilities.swift:31-45). This is the signal that makes 'which of my 100 skills matter' answerable. |

Provenance also gates edit/archive on desktop. |

<a id="settings"></a>

### Settings

`settings` · 202 rows — ✅ 20 · 🔶 31 · ⭕ 121 · ➖ 30

Desktop Settings is a 13-entry overlay (8 schema-driven config sections + Notifications, Billing, Providers, Gateway, Keybinds, Keys, Plugins, Archived Chats, About) carrying ~180 distinct user-visible controls: ~70 config.yaml keys autosaved through REST `PUT /api/config`, a full model-assignment surface (main model, 8 auxiliary task slots, Mixture-of-Agents presets), a provider/API-key/custom-endpoint catalog, a 4-mode gateway connection editor plus a named-connections registry, per-device appearance/notification/quick-entry prefs, plugin management, session retention, billing, and updates/uninstall. Talaria has NO settings screen at all — the entire surface is one `ConnectionsView` with three sections: a 3-theme swatch picker, the saved-gateway list (connect/rename/sign-out/remove/add + Hermes Cloud discovery), and a notification block. The three real strengths are gateway management (genuinely at parity, arguably better: Tailscale/Cloud/URL modes, Keychain-backed creds, PKCE + device-code flows), push notifications (a real APNs registration card desktop has no analogue for), and per-session model/reasoning/YOLO pickers. The biggest holes, in value order: (1) there is no config editor of any kind — a phone user cannot change approval mode, memory, compression, delegation, toolsets, safety, or timeouts, only per-session model/effort/yolo; (2) the "Notify me when" rows are DEMO DATA (`DemoData.notificationPrefs`) that toggle local state and are never persisted or sent to the relay — this is a live parity lie, not a gap; (3) no provider/API-key surface, so an expired key strands the phone entirely; (4) no auxiliary-model or MoA control, so a main-model switch silently leaves aux tasks billing a dead provider with no way to see or fix it. Highest-ROI first moves: wire the notification prefs to the relay (S, and it fixes a correctness bug), a generic schema-driven config editor over `GET /api/config/schema` + `PUT /api/config` (L, unlocks ~70 rows at once), and an API-key sheet over `GET/PUT /api/env` + `model.save_key` (M).

> **Audit note.** The summary's interim tally was written against a partial pass; the real counts for this area are on the line above. Two rows were corrected on 2026-08-18: `voice.auto_tts` and `voice.max_recording_seconds` are no longer ⭕ — Talaria has a persisted auto-speak toggle and a 45 s recording cap, both device-local rather than gateway config. The summary's central claim is unchanged and is the single most important sentence in this document: **there is no settings screen**, and the notification preference rows that do exist are demo data that persist nothing.

| Feature | Desktop source | Surface | Talaria | Effort · value | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Settings screen exists at all (dedicated entry point with sectioned nav) | `app/settings/index.tsx:155-274` | client-only (route overlay); sections fetch REST/WS | ✅ `TalariaUI/Screens/SettingsView.swift:28` | M · critical | Landed 2026-08-18. Nine sections — Gateways, Appearance, Notifications, Models, Voice, Solo, Privacy & data, About, and an honest desktop pointer — reached from the roster header and from Connections through `AppModel.requestSettings()`. Deliberately not desktop's 202 controls: ROADMAP decision 1 keeps raw config and env editing off the phone. |

<a id="profiles-botmode"></a>

### Profiles & Bot Mode shell

`profiles-botmode` · 111 rows — ✅ 20 · 🔶 23 · ⭕ 62 · ➖ 6

Desktop's profile surface is three stacked products: (1) the /profiles management overlay + the sidebar Profile Rail (CRUD, SOUL.md editing, per-profile color, drag-reorder, export/import, 5 keybinds, hover pre-warm); (2) the in-tree `hermes-bots` Bot Mode plugin (8323 lines) — a Discord-shaped roster with pinning, unread watermarks, an Active-now strip, per-bot canonical "forever chats", animated shape/color/image/pet avatars, a full per-profile capability editor (model picker, skills + Skills Hub install, toolsets w/ config panels, MCP w/ OAuth + API-key setup), group chats with a round-robin turn loop, @mention autocomplete + handoff middleware, and multi-gateway agent federation; (3) the Command Center (sessions/system/usage/maintenance) plus the Pet Generate overlay. Talaria covers the *identity and configuration* core genuinely well: profiles.list → roster with desktop ui_meta title/shape/color honored (AppModelLive.swift:173), a dirty-diff profile editor that refuses to write sections it could not read (CreateBotView.swift:689), duplicate, portrait generation via image.generate → profiles.set_asset, and a per-profile CapabilitiesView that is arguably *stronger* than the desktop checklist for MCP (add/remove/probe/OAuth/API key). The gaps are concentrated in five places: (a) profile lifecycle — no rename, no delete, no export/import, no per-profile color; (b) roster affordances — no context menu at all, so pin, group, duplicate-from-roster, per-bot Sessions and delete have no entry point, and no unread/active-now/DM-preview signals; (c) the bot-to-bot layer — no canonical forever-chat pinning, no group chats, no @mention autocomplete or handoff middleware (only a manual A2A compose sheet), no multi-gateway agent federation in the roster; (d) avatar authoring — no upload, no custom-prompt generation, no pet gallery, and generated portraits never appear on roster rows; (e) the entire Command Center — no usage analytics, no log tail, no gateway restart/update, no doctor/backup/curator/memory ops — and the entire Pet Generate flow. Highest-leverage fixes for a phone-first power user: a roster long-press menu (unlocks 6 desktop actions at once, M), unread + active-now signals from profiles.list watermarks (M), a Capabilities entry point on the bot sheet (S), and delete-profile (S, REST already exists).

Needs the REST verb, which Talaria's GatewayREST does not yet wrap. |

A phone-only user therefore cannot ever remove a bot they created. |

<a id="cron-agents-artifacts"></a>

### Cron, subagents & artifacts

`cron-agents-artifacts` · 101 rows — ✅ 14 · 🔶 27 · ⭕ 55 · ➖ 5

Granular 1:1 audit of Hermes Desktop's four "background work" surfaces — /cron (scheduled jobs), /agents (subagent monitor), /artifacts (output gallery), /starmap + learning-node mutations — against Talaria's RoutinesView, ArtifactsView and the feeds runtime. Headline: (1) CRON is the only area with real coverage — Talaria genuinely does list/create/pause/resume/trigger/delete via cron.manage + the REST trigger, with a client-side natural-language schedule normalizer desktop lacks. But everything past the basic verbs is absent: no edit, no run history, no delivery targets, no per-job model override, no blueprints, no last_error, no prompt display, no search, no state pills beyond a binary toggle, and no live next-run countdown. Talaria's routines are also structurally per-bot (pushed from a bot chat, "[bot:] " title namespacing) where desktop's is a flat per-profile job manager — a different mental model, not just fewer buttons. (2) AGENTS is a total hole: `subagent.*` events are not in Talaria's TypedGatewayEvent switch at all, `Usage.activeSubagents` is parsed and never rendered, so a phone driving a delegating agent gets zero visibility into spawn trees, live stream tails, per-child token/cost, or the ability to jump into a child session. This is the single highest-value gap in the area for a phone-first power user. (3) ARTIFACTS is a faithful shell with an honest client-side extraction port, but it stops at metadata: images are decorative diagonal-stripe placeholders (no bytes ever fetched), there is no lightbox, no copy-path/copy-URL, no open-externally/download, no in-tab search, no pagination, and the extractor omits Windows paths, `<untrusted_tool_result>` unwrapping and structured multimodal `meta`. Tapping a card correctly opens the exact producing session — that part is done well. (4) STARMAP/LEARNING is entirely absent (the BotSheetView "star map" is a decorative twinkle field whose only live input is a skill+session count), and with it the node edit / skill-archive / memory-delete mutations and the HML share-code export/import. Reasonable to deprioritize the canvas viz on a phone, but the learning.frames/detail/edit/delete WS quartet is a mobile-shaped list path that would recover the curation actions cheaply.

<a id="messaging-webhooks-overlays"></a>

### Messaging, webhooks & overlays

`messaging-webhooks-overlays` · 105 rows — ✅ 6 · 🔶 20 · ⭕ 54 · ➖ 25

Desktop's messaging/webhooks/overlay/palette/quick-entry/HUD surfaces are, with one narrow exception, entirely absent from Talaria. (1) Messaging-platform onboarding and pairing management (apps/desktop/src/app/messaging/index.tsx, 933 lines over REST /api/messaging/platforms + /api/pairing) has ZERO counterpart: no platform list, no credential editor, no enable toggle, and — most consequentially for a phone-first operator — no pairing inbox, so a stranger DM'ing your Telegram bot can be neither seen nor approved/revoked from the phone. Talaria already decodes `platforms.changed` and `pairing.changed` off the socket (TalariaKit/GatewayEvents.swift:101) and then drops them on the floor (AppModelLive.swift:506), so the event plumbing is half-built with no consumer. (2) Webhooks (apps/desktop/src/app/webhooks/index.tsx, 609 lines over /api/webhooks) has zero counterpart — no enable, no subscription CRUD, no one-time-secret reveal; Talaria's REST vocabulary (grep of all /api/ paths in Sources) contains neither /api/messaging, /api/pairing, nor /api/webhooks. (3) The ⌘K command palette is the one place with real overlap: Talaria's SearchPalette.swift searches bots/sessions/artifacts and offers ~9 action chips, and its server-backed session FTS is arguably better than desktop's client-side filter over a recent-200 list — but it lacks ranking, nested pages, settings/field/MCP/archived rows, theme+mode pickers, marketplace install, pets, projects, worktree branches, plugin contributions, live-state toggles, restart-gateway and update rows. Talaria's separate CommandPaletteSheet.swift is a slash-command sheet, a different surface from desktop's ⌘K. (4) Overlay chrome exists in spirit (push transitions, sheets, scrim-tap) but there is no updates overlay at all — no version check, changelog, apply progress, manual-command, GUI-skew or blocker flow — which is defensible since iOS ships through the App Store, though the BACKEND update target (desktop's remote-mode "update the gateway" path) has no mobile door and is a genuine gap. (5) Quick Entry and (6) HUD mode are structurally desktop-shaped (global OS accelerator, transparent always-on-top click-through window) and mostly na-mobile, but their INTENT — capture a prompt without opening the app — maps cleanly onto iOS App Intents / Shortcuts / a Lock Screen or Control Center widget / share sheet, and Talaria implements none of those (TalariaWidgets ships only a Live Activity; grep for AppIntent/keyboardShortcut across Packages+ios returns nothing). Highest-value gaps for a solo operator on a phone, in order: pairing approve/revoke, messaging platform enable + credential entry, restart-gateway action, webhook subscription CRUD, and an App-Intents quick-capture surface.

Grep of every /api/ path used across Packages/Talaria/Sources returns no /api/messaging. Includes the list column, filter, and the detail pane scaffolding. |

<a id="gateway-store-hooks"></a>

### Gateway, store & hooks

`gateway-store-hooks` · 83 rows — ✅ 29 · 🔶 24 · ⭕ 22 · ➖ 8

Talaria's transport core is genuinely strong and in places better-reasoned than desktop's: JSON-RPC id correlation, per-call timeouts, connect handshake gated on gateway.ready, pending-call rejection on socket death, OAuth refresh + single-use ticket minting per dial, an event-pump-completion disconnect signal, exponential backoff, durable-key resume inside the ~20s park window with in-flight/approval/clarify replay, sid invalidation on reconnect, session.reclaimed handling, a supervised reconnect layer with a re-auth banner and foreground recovery, and a saved-gateway registry with Keychain-only credentials and /api/status health probes. The parity gaps cluster in four places. (1) Client-side observability: desktop's whole in-app toast feed, error normalization/summaries, agent `notification.show` notices (credit/cost-cap warnings), billing-wall recovery, and MCP health sweeps have no Talaria equivalent — Talaria surfaces errors only as system chat bubbles or a themed one-liner, and its per-kind notification preference rows are demo data that toggle in memory and gate nothing. (2) Reconnect completeness: no network-online reconnect trigger (foreground only), no `session.active_list` re-seed so which sessions are running/waiting after a reconnect is guesswork and stuck spinners never get reaped, no bounded boot retry, and the offline compose queue lives in a plain in-memory array that dies with the app and cannot carry attachments. (3) The multi-socket registry is entirely absent — Talaria binds one gateway at a time, so a second saved gateway's running agents go dark the moment you switch, and there is no scope-keyed pooling, prewarm, or liveness-bounded pruning. (4) Caching/refresh discipline: no shared query cache with profile-scoped invalidation, no `change_events` capability gate (it is parsed and then ignored), no visibility/battery-aware poll cadence, and no transcript-signature reconcile. The keybind registry is legitimately na-mobile (portrait iPhone, no hardware-keyboard bindings), but its command-palette door exists. Highest-value fixes for a phone-first operator, in order: persist the compose queue, add `session.active_list` re-seed + reap, add a real in-app notice/toast surface wired to `notification.show`, add network-path reconnect, and honor `change_events`.

NWPathMonitor with a settle delay and a nudge floor, so a flapping interface cannot dial in a loop. A path that comes back while the socket survived reconciles instead of redialling. The mechanism compiles and is wired from `startLivenessSupervision`, but no cell/Wi-Fi handoff has been exercised. |

<a id="electron-native"></a>

### Electron & native shell

`electron-native` · 154 rows — ✅ 19 · 🔶 9 · ⭕ 57 · ➖ 69

> **Current correction:** the paragraph below is the 2026-08-17 snapshot. Since
> then Talaria added artifact download/preview/share, source-qualified Git and
> Files controls, remote Hermes update/restart operations, and authenticated
> Advanced Terminal. Rich generated-media presentation and transcript search
> remain real gaps; use the current audit for the complete list.

Exhaustive audit of every user-visible main-process capability in apps/desktop/electron (205 files, main.ts alone is 15,201 lines) against Talaria's native iOS client. The honest headline: this area splits cleanly into three buckets. (1) Genuinely na-mobile — multi-window management (instance/session/watch windows, geometry persistence, fullscreen, traffic lights, titlebar overlay, translucency, always-on-top), the HUD floating chat bar and its 6 sub-behaviors, the pet overlay and its 6 sub-behaviors, the wake-indicator window, global accelerators (Quick Entry, ⌘⇧G HUD snap), DevTools, the OS menu bar and its ~15 items, ⌘W tab-close interception, spellcheck context menu, keep-awake, quit guard, self-update/uninstall, single-instance lock, external terminal launch, read-window-below, and Finder/Explorer reveal. For most of these iOS has a real analogue that Talaria has NOT built (Share Extension / App Intent / Control Center widget for Quick Entry; Dynamic Type for zoom; home-screen widget for the pet; Files-app "Open in" for reveal), so "na-mobile" here means "the desktop mechanism is impossible", not "there is nothing to build". (2) Genuinely present — OS notifications with actionable Approve/Later, notification tap→bot routing, APNs relay registration, deep-link scheme with 3 hosts, mic permission, file/photo/clipboard attach, external URL open, in-app OAuth webview. Talaria's push stack is arguably ahead of desktop's (service extension, Live Activity, communication-style notifications). (3) The real gaps that are mobile-viable and missing — the ENTIRE git suite (20 operations: worktrees, branches, review list/diff, stage/unstage/revert, commit, push, PR create/list/comment, repo scan), which the gateway already mirrors at REST `/api/git/*` (hermes_cli/web_routers/git.py:33-194) so nothing structural blocks it; the interactive terminal, which the gateway already serves at `WS /api/pty`; artifact/file download-and-save (Talaria's Artifacts tab only jumps to chat, it cannot open or save a single produced file); the `hermes-media://` streaming protocol, so no agent-produced image, audio or video is viewable on the phone at all; find-in-transcript; and remote `hermes update` fan-out. Those five are where phone-first parity is actually lost, and none of them are blocked by iOS.

| Feature | Desktop source | Surface | Talaria | Effort · value | Notes |

Talaria has an MCP add-server sheet (Screens/CapabilitiesView.swift:1143) but nothing routes a URL into it, so the vendor one-tap install button is dead on the phone. |

A phone analogue is a sheet/tab, not a window — nothing structural blocks it, but Talaria has no subagent watch surface at all. |

On iOS the analogue is a Live Activity phase or an in-app overlay — buildable, just absent. |

Talaria's SessionsSheet (Screens/SessionsSheet.swift:730) can pick a session but only inside the app after you've already opened it. |

---

<a id="mobile-ceiling"></a>

## Mobile ceiling

189 of the 1,190 rows are marked ➖: they describe desktop behaviour that cannot cross to iOS, or that would be a category error on a phone. They are listed here in full, each with why it stops and what the iOS analogue is, so that a future reader does not re-open them as bugs.

The reasons cluster into six kinds:

- **Pointer-only affordances** — hover tooltips, hover-reveal action pills, right-click-precision context menus, drag handles that assume a cursor. The touch analogue is almost always long-press or an explicit disclosure, and where Talaria already has one the row says so.
- **Multi-window and window placement** — new-window cascade, HUD windows, watch windows, per-display Spotlight-style centering, show/hide a second sidebar. iOS gives one full-screen scene per app on iPhone; the analogue is a sheet or a pushed screen, which changes the interaction model rather than porting it.
- **Local process ownership** — the desktop *hosts* the Hermes agent: it installs a bundled Python tree, supervises the gateway process, restarts it, reports its PID and log tail. Talaria talks to a gateway it does not own, so these rows have no phone-side referent — the nearest analogue is gateway pairing and health probing in Connections.
- **Hardware-keyboard paths** — bare-letter hotkeys, ⌘-chords, ↓/↑/Enter list navigation, a rebindable shortcut editor. Real on iPad with a keyboard and unreachable on iPhone. Talaria registers no `.keyboardShortcut` anywhere today, so these are *deferred*, not impossible — see the caveat below.
- **Filesystem and OS integration** — reveal-in-Finder, native file dialogs pointing at arbitrary local paths, custom URL protocol handlers for local media, and Screen Recording permission gates remain desktop-local. Remote gateway files and PTY are portable and now have Command Center equivalents; document picker, share sheet, and Quick Look cover device-local exchange.
- **Structurally moot on this shell** — options that only make sense against desktop's information architecture — grouping sessions by profile when the roster *is* the profile list, or a setting whose only consumer is a surface the phone does not have (terminal font, xterm theme).

Two caveats worth carrying forward. First, ➖ is a judgement about *this* form factor: several rows marked "no hardware keyboard" become real on an iPad with a Magic Keyboard, and Talaria registers no `.keyboardShortcut` anywhere today — that is a genuine iPad gap hiding inside an iPhone non-gap. Second, "the desktop hosts the agent locally" is the single largest generator of ➖ rows: Talaria talks to a gateway it does not own, so process supervision, bundled-Python installs, local file protocols and window management have no phone-side referent at all.

| Feature | Area | Desktop source | Why it stops at the phone / iOS analogue |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| Cards that must stay visible (diff, clarify, image, delegate) are excluded from run collapsing | `chat-transcript` | `components/assistant-ui/tool/fallback.tsx:751-783 (splitRunItems / isCardTool)` | Meaningless until run collapsing exists; listed because it is the rule that makes collapsing safe and must land with it. |

<a id="roadmap"></a>

## Historical roadmap — superseded 25-gap snapshot

> [!CAUTION]
> This ranked list is retained only as audit provenance. Items such as Settings,
> `session.active_list`, reconnect reaping, multiline mention composition, cron
> history/editing, pairing administration, and Advanced Terminal have since
> landed. Use `docs/PARITY-BASELINE.md` plus `docs/HERMES-CURRENT-AUDIT.md` for
> current priorities; do not implement from the list below without re-auditing.

Ranked by **value × (1/effort)**, over ⭕ and 🔶 rows only. Value weights are `critical 8 / high 4 / medium 2 / low 1`; effort divisors are `S 1 / M 2 / L 4 / XL 8`; a ⭕ scores 15% above a 🔶 of the same size, because a hole costs more than a rough edge. Ties — and there are many, since the scale is coarse — are broken by **dependency order** (things that unblock other rows first) and by **phone-first leverage** (things whose value is *higher* on a phone than on a desktop).

Each entry names the concrete first step. Where a row is 🔶, the step is the specific half that is missing, not a rewrite.

### Next (1–10)

The first nine are all S-sized and critical; together they are a few days of work and they fix the things that make Talaria feel *unowned* — a session you cannot pin, a network that comes back and nothing happens, an MCP server you installed that the agent cannot see. Number ten is the keystone: it is M-sized, and 132 other ⭕ rows are waiting behind it.

#### 1. ⭕ Reload MCP into live sessions after any config write (`reload.mcp`)

`capabilities-skills-mcp` · **S · critical** · desktop `app/skills/mcp-tab.tsx:738-749,757-769`

Talaria can install, enable and credential an MCP server from the phone — and the running agent never finds out. Desktop calls `silentReload()` after *every* persist (add, remove, enable, per-tool change, OAuth success, catalog install), and the inline setup card fires it before unblocking the agent that asked for the server. Without it, the entire Capabilities screen is write-to-disk-and-hope: the user does the work, sees a success state, and the agent still says the tool does not exist.

**First step —** Add `reloadMCP(sessionID:)` to `GatewayClient+Capabilities.swift` sending `reload.mcp {confirm: true, session_id}`, then call it from the tail of every mutation in `AppModelLive+Capabilities.swift` (the same set desktop reloads after). One RPC, one call site per mutation, no UI.

#### 2. ⭕ Reconnect nudge when the OS reports the network came back online

`gateway-store-hooks` · **S · critical** · desktop `app/gateway/hooks/use-gateway-boot.ts:547`

A phone changes networks constantly — Wi-Fi to cellular, dead zone to signal, airplane mode off. Today none of that wakes the socket: Talaria sits out its exponential backoff (up to 30 s) while the network has been fine for twenty seconds, and the only user workaround is to background and foreground the app. Desktop nudges the reconnect the moment the OS reports online, which on a laptop is a nicety and on a phone is the difference between 'instant' and 'broken'.

**First step —** Add an `NWPathMonitor` to `AppModelLive+Reconnect.swift`; on a transition to `.satisfied`, cancel the pending backoff sleep and retry immediately. Nothing else in the reconnect ladder needs to change — it already handles a retry arriving early.

#### 3. ⭕ Pin / unpin a session from the row's kebab or context menu

`sidebars-nav` · **S · critical** · desktop `app/chat/sidebar/session-actions-menu.tsx:305-312`

There is no pin concept in Talaria at all: grep `pinned` across `Sources/` and you get `pinnedModel` and `toolsetsPinned`, both unrelated. For someone driving three or four long-running agents from a phone, the recents list is a churn of everything, and the two conversations that matter sink below the fold within a day. This row is the cheap half of the pinning story (#15 is the section it feeds).

**First step —** Add `pinned` to the session row model and a Pin/Unpin action to `SessionsSheet.swift`'s existing swipe + `contextMenu` (which today offer only Rename and Delete), writing `PATCH /api/sessions/<id> {pinned}` through the REST context `GatewayClient+Cron.swift` already uses for cron triggers.

#### 4. ⭕ Pending-pairing count badge on the platform list row ("someone is waiting to be let in")

`messaging-webhooks-overlays` · **S · critical** · desktop `app/messaging/index.tsx:512`

When an unknown person DMs a bot on Discord or Telegram they are blocked pending approval, and the only signal that anyone is waiting is a count badge on the platform row — which Talaria has neither the badge nor the endpoint for. This is the pre-open signal for the highest-value messaging flow (#19/#20), and on a phone it is exactly the sort of thing that should eventually be a push notification rather than something you have to go look for.

**First step —** Add `GET /api/pairing` to the REST client and surface `pending.count` per platform as a badge on the messaging list row. Fetch on appear plus on the existing foreground refresh; do not poll.

#### 5. 🔶 Capabilities editor reachable from a specific bot (not just 'the current profile')

`profiles-botmode` · **S · critical** · desktop `plugins/hermes-bots/plugin.js:5106`

`openCapabilities` resolves `model.openBotID ?? defaultCapabilityProfile`, so opening Capabilities from the roster — where no chat is open — silently edits the **default** profile instead of the bot you were looking at. The screen itself is per-profile-correct throughout; only the entry point loses the scope. Worse, the bot sheet has no Capabilities row at all, so the correct door does not exist.

**First step —** Add a Capabilities row to `BotSheetView.swift` that passes that bot's profile explicitly, and make `RootView.swift:341` refuse to fall back to the default profile when it has no `openBotID` — show the picker instead of guessing.

#### 6. 🔶 Session picker overlay opened by /resume, /sessions, /switch slash commands

`sidebars-nav` · **S · critical** · desktop `app/session-picker-overlay.tsx:15-32; components/session-picker.tsx`

`SessionsSheet` is a good session picker that resumes correctly — and almost nothing opens it. It hangs off one `SearchPalette` action; the `.talariaOpenSessions` notification `RootView` listens for has **no producer anywhere in the repo**, and `/resume`, `/sessions` and `/switch` do not route to it. A working surface with no door is indistinguishable from a missing feature.

**First step —** Post `.talariaOpenSessions` from three places: the chat header, the `/resume`-family commands in `AppModelLive+Commands.swift`, and the command palette. The sheet needs no changes.

#### 7. 🔶 Sidebar nav row: New session (creates a fresh chat in the active profile)

`sidebars-nav` · **S · critical** · desktop `app/chat/sidebar/index.tsx:188-194,1484-1494`

You cannot deliberately start a fresh conversation with an existing bot. `openChat` always resumes the most recent stored session, and `createSession` is reached only when no stored session exists — so a long, polluted context is a one-way door. On desktop this is a permanent nav row.

**First step —** Add a 'New session' action to the chat header and the roster row menu that calls the existing `createSession` path unconditionally, rather than only as a no-stored-session fallback (`AppModelLive.swift:235-246,265-275`).

#### 8. 🔶 Approval mode: manual / smart / off (approvals.mode)

`settings` · **S · critical** · desktop `app/settings/constants.ts:665, 233-235, 574-576`

Talaria's YOLO card is a per-bot binary that writes `config.set {key: 'yolo', scope: 'session'}`. Desktop's is a three-way persisted policy — manual / smart / off — written at global scope as `approvals.mode`. The consequence is that a phone user's auto-approve choice evaporates with the session, and 'smart' (approve the safe class, ask about the rest), which is the only setting a sane person leaves on unattended, is unreachable.

**First step —** Extend the YOLO control in `BotSheetView.swift:362` to three states and send `scope: 'global'` — the gateway already accepts it (`ws-protocol.md:524-529`), so this is a parameter change plus a segmented control.

#### 9. 🔶 REST fallback when the gateway answers with an empty/partial catalog

`models-providers` · **S · critical** · desktop `lib/model-options.ts:87-114`

When a live gateway answers with an empty or partial catalog, Talaria falls back to `DemoData.models` — against a **live** gateway. It offers fabricated ids like `Hermes-4-405B (default)` and `grok-4 · api` that fail on selection. `modelChoices()` already guards against exactly this (`AppModelLive+Profiles.swift:196-200`); `availableModels()` and the picker do not. Desktop retries over REST instead.

**First step —** Make `availableModels()` return empty rather than `DemoData.models` when `mode == .live`, and have `ModelEffortSheet` render its existing no-providers empty state. Add the `GET /api/model/options` REST retry after that, as a second commit.

#### 10. ⭕ Settings screen exists at all (dedicated entry point with sectioned nav)

`settings` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/settings/index.tsx:155-274`

Desktop's settings overlay has 13 sections and 202 discrete controls. Talaria has no settings screen, no gear, no tab: the only settings-ish surface is `ConnectionsView`, reached by tapping the net chip on the roster, and it holds theme, gateways and notifications. This single absence is why the Settings area reads as 130 ⭕ rows — they are not 130 decisions, they are one, and everything downstream is blocked on it.

**First step —** Add a Settings destination (tab or roster-header gear) with a sectioned list, and move the existing theme / gateways / notifications cards out of `ConnectionsView` into it as the first three sections. Land the shell empty rather than waiting to fill it — every later settings row becomes an S once there is somewhere to put it.

### Then (11–25)

Mostly M-sized. Two clusters dominate: **liveness after suspension** (11–14), which is the difference between trusting a backgrounded agent and not; and **the surfaces that turn Talaria from a viewer into an administrator** — cron editing and run history, pairing approvals, credentials, and a composer you can write a paragraph in.

#### 11. ⭕ Reap runtimes that vanish between active_list snapshots — clears stuck spinners and fires the 'your turn' unread edge

`gateway-store-hooks` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/contrib/hooks/use-background-sync.ts:495`

The highest-severity liveness bug in the app. If a turn finishes while Talaria is backgrounded, its `message.complete` is never delivered, and **nothing else clears the state** — the bot stays pinned to `.working` forever, spinner and all. Desktop reaps runtimes that vanish between `session.active_list` snapshots and uses that same edge to fire the 'your turn' unread signal.

**First step —** Ships with #12: after the `active_list` re-seed lands, diff the previous snapshot against the new one and clear any session that disappeared, raising the unread edge as you do.

#### 12. ⭕ Re-seed which sessions are running/waiting after a reconnect via `session.active_list`

`gateway-store-hooks` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/contrib/hooks/use-background-sync.ts:467`

Talaria only learns a bot is working from a live event or a resume ack's `running` flag. Every turn that started while the phone was asleep shows idle on the roster until the next event happens to land — which for a long tool run can be minutes. `session.active_list` is one RPC that answers 'what is actually running right now' authoritatively.

**First step —** Call `session.active_list` at the end of the reconnect ladder in `AppModelLive+Reconnect.swift` and project the result onto roster status, before any event replay is processed.

#### 13. ⭕ Agent notices from `notification.show` / `notification.clear` (credit warnings, cost caps) with sticky vs TTL lifetime and severity ramp

`gateway-store-hooks` · **M · critical** · desktop `store/agent-notices.ts:21`

`notification.show` and `notification.clear` are already decoded into `TypedGatewayEvent` (`GatewayEvents.swift:87`) and then dropped on the floor — there is no consumer. These carry credit warnings and cost caps, so running out of credits mid-turn is invisible on the phone until the turn errors out with something unhelpful.

**First step —** Add an agent-notice store with desktop's two lifetimes (sticky vs TTL) and render it in the existing `PushBanner` slot. The decode already exists; this is a consumer and a view.

#### 14. ⭕ Multi-line composer that grows with the draft

`chat-transcript` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/chat/composer/index.tsx:1010-1068`

The composer is a fixed 42 pt single-line `TextField`. An agent instruction is normally a paragraph, and you can neither see nor edit one — you type into a slot that shows eight words at a time. This is the most-felt ergonomic gap in the app and it is upstream of several others (draft persistence, @-mentions, the edit composer).

**First step —** Replace the `TextField` at `ChatView.swift:724-734` with a `TextEditor`-backed growing field capped at ~5 lines with internal scroll, keeping the existing `onSubmit` send and the `/` palette trigger.

#### 15. ⭕ Pinned sessions section (collapsible, always above recents, excluded from every other list)

`sidebars-nav` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/chat/sidebar/index.tsx:1596-1618,550-597`

The section that makes #3 worth having: pinned sessions collapsible above recents and excluded from every other list, so the two conversations you actually run stay put while the rest churn. For a phone operator with three or four long-running agents this is the single highest-value list change available.

**First step —** Once `pinned` exists (#3), add a pinned group to `SessionsSheet` above the recents query and filter pinned ids out of the recents list. Hand-picked ordering is a later, separate row.

#### 16. ⭕ Run history: last N run sessions for a job, newest first, with per-run timestamp

`cron-agents-artifacts` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/cron/index.tsx:847,869-956; hermes.ts:1570-1578`

There is no run history anywhere. The nearest thing is the Activity ledger, which journals a single line per newly-observed `last_run` after a `cron.changed` refresh — no list, no backfill, no per-run identity. The number one thing a phone user wants from cron is proof that the 7am job ran and what it produced, and today the app cannot answer it.

**First step —** Add `GET /api/cron/jobs/{id}/runs?limit=` to the REST client and render the last N runs, newest first, in the job detail on `RoutinesView`.

#### 17. ⭕ Click a run in the history to open that run's session transcript

`cron-agents-artifacts` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/cron/index.tsx:939-951`

A run history you cannot open is a list of timestamps. Desktop makes each run a door into that run's session transcript, which is where the actual answer to 'what did it do' lives.

**First step —** Mostly wiring once #16 lands: map each run to a `SessionRef` and reuse `AppModel.open(SessionRef)` (`AppModelLive+Feeds.swift:730-739`), which already resumes an arbitrary stored session.

#### 18. ⭕ Edit an existing job — reopen the same dialog prefilled with name/prompt/schedule/deliver/model and PUT the changes

`cron-agents-artifacts` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/cron/index.tsx:588-608,677,1089-1104; app/cron/cron-job-model.ts:72-94`

The row menu offers Run now and Delete. There is no edit sheet, so fixing a typo in a scheduled prompt means deleting the job and recreating it from scratch on a phone keyboard — which is also how you lose its history.

**First step —** Reuse the create sheet, prefilled, and `PUT /api/cron/jobs/{id}` — note that `cron.manage` exposes no update action, so this must go over REST, which the app already does for triggers (the `gatewayRESTContext` credential plumbing exists).

#### 19. ⭕ Pending pairing requests list per platform, with requester name/id and wait age ("just now" / "12m ago")

`messaging-webhooks-overlays` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/messaging/index.tsx:586, :592 (waitingSince)`

An unknown person DMing a bot is blocked until an admin approves them, and today that admin has to be at a desktop. Being able to unblock a colleague from your phone in ten seconds is the clearest 'the phone is enough' story in this entire document.

**First step —** `GET /api/pairing` → render `pending[]` per platform with requester name/id and a relative wait age. Builds directly on #4's fetch.

#### 20. ⭕ Approve a pairing request (optimistic row removal, snapshot rollback on failure, dedicated 429 brute-force-lockout message)

`messaging-webhooks-overlays` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/messaging/index.tsx:354 (handleApprove), :375 (429 branch)`

The action half of #19. Desktop removes the row optimistically, rolls back from a snapshot on failure, and has a dedicated message for the 429 brute-force lockout — that last one matters because a pairing endpoint is exactly what gets hammered.

**First step —** `POST /api/pairing/approve {platform, request_id, profile}`. Approve on **`request_id`, never the pairing code** — the code is the requester's proof and the API never returns it. Port the 429 branch with its own copy.

#### 21. ⭕ @mention autocomplete in the composer, offering roster handles with the bot's display name as meta

`profiles-botmode` · **M · critical** · desktop `plugins/hermes-bots/plugin.js:8002`

Bot-to-bot handoffs are addressed by profile slug, and on a phone keyboard nobody types an exact slug correctly. Without completion the entire delegation layer is effectively unreachable from the composer — the feature exists and cannot be invoked.

**First step —** After #14's growing composer lands, add an `@` trigger that filters the cached `profiles.list` and inserts the slug, showing the display name as meta. Client-only; no gateway work. (Deeper mention semantics are specified in `docs/BOT-MODE-PARITY.md`.)

#### 22. ⭕ API keys: set / replace a credential value

`settings` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/settings/env-credentials.tsx:91-138; credential-key-ui.tsx:1`

Talaria stores *gateway* credentials in the Keychain and never writes a backend env var. That means a provider whose key expired cannot be fixed from the phone at all — the agent is dead until you reach a laptop. Desktop treats this as a two-field form.

**First step —** Add `PUT /api/env` to the REST client and a credential row that writes one key, with the value field cleared after save and the current value shown only as a redacted placeholder.

#### 23. ⭕ Activate an unconfigured API-key provider inline (paste key, auto-pick default model)

`settings` · **M · critical** · desktop `app/settings/model-settings.tsx:774-798, 542-585`

The fastest path from 'provider dead' to 'working' on desktop: paste a key, the backend picks a sensible default model, and the next turn runs. It is the same plumbing as #22 plus one recommended-model call, and it turns a dead-end error into a 20-second fix.

**First step —** Chain `model.save_key` (already ported at `GatewayClient+Models.swift:531-540` with no caller) to `GET /api/model/recommended-default`, and offer it from the picker whenever a provider row reports `authenticated: false` — the picker already parses `auth_type` and `key_env`.

#### 24. ⭕ Composer draft persists per session across navigation

`chat-transcript` · **S · high** · desktop `app/chat/composer/hooks/use-composer-draft.ts:1-505`

The draft is plain `@State` on `ChatView`. Backgrounding the app or popping to the roster — both of which happen constantly on a phone, often involuntarily — discards a half-typed prompt. Desktop persists drafts per session.

**First step —** Move `draft` into the per-session `ChatState` and persist it to `UserDefaults` keyed by session id, restoring on appear. Lands naturally with #14.

#### 25. ⭕ Effort control seeded from the session's actual reasoning level

`models-providers` · **S · high** · desktop `app/shell/model-menu-panel.tsx:63,184-189; app/session/hooks/use-hermes-config.ts:90-106`

The effort sheet seeds from `ChatState.reasoningEffort`, which starts empty and is only ever written by the user's own tap. So a freshly opened sheet shows a level the agent may not be using — the UI states something it does not know. Everything else about the picker was fixed in the 2026-08-17 pass; this is the last lie in it.

**First step —** Read the session's real level on sheet open (`config.get {key: 'reasoning'}`, falling back to the profile's `agent.reasoning_effort`) and seed the chips from it instead of from local state.

---

## Maintaining this document

- **A row is the unit.** When a behaviour lands, flip its glyph, fill in `talariaSource` with the real `file:line`, and append `landed <date>` to the note. Do not delete rows — a ✅ row is the regression test description for the thing you just shipped.
- **Do not trust a stale status.** This document was written from an audit that was partly stale within hours; the corrections above are dated for exactly that reason. Before quoting a ⭕ at someone, grep `Sources/` for it.
- **➖ is a decision, not a status.** Moving a row out of ➖ needs a stated reason (usually: iPad, or the gateway grew a route that makes a local-only behaviour remote). Moving a row *into* ➖ needs the analogue named, or it is just a deferral wearing a costume.
- **Bot Mode rows live in [`docs/BOT-MODE-PARITY.md`](docs/BOT-MODE-PARITY.md).** If a behaviour is about the roster, canonical chat, handles, delegation or group rooms, it belongs there, not here.
